      - generic: player = """# Banishment — Repair 03 Provisional Player Procedure
      - text: "## Scope and source identity This is a **NEW provisional authored playtest procedure** for the ordinary rank-4 **Banishment** spell only. It does not alter the accepted AC4 fifteen-spell module, choose a canonical plane, settle soul/origin identity, or merge with Cleric Banish Unholy."
      - text: "Preserved source identity: **Abjuration; rank 4; base 7 RP; Action; 60 feet; V/S/F; “Send a creature to another plane temporarily.”** The Core source also preserves **Instantaneous** metadata. Because that source does not itself provide a runnable temporary duration, this candidate keeps that metadata as evidence and adds an explicitly NEW concentration limit of **60 elapsed seconds**."
      - text: "## Casting and save Choose one creature you actually perceive within 60 feet and line of effect. A corpse, image, soul record or unattended object is not a creature target."
      - text: "Before the save, record the target's **admission intent**: - **ACCEPT** — the creature is voluntarily accepting this extradimensional displacement. It may elect failure or roll; if it rolls and fails, the admission is still voluntary. - **RESIST** — a failed save produces forced displacement."
      - text: Commit the Action, V/S/F, actual final RP price and concentration normally. A willing target may elect to fail before rolling. Otherwise the target makes the NEW Banishment **Charisma save** against the actual spell DC. Actual save features apply, including Fate-Bound's exact +2 against banishment.
      - text: A failed save can establish exile only if the existing shared hard-control allowance for that source-character/target/encounter is available. Only one live ordinary Banishment application may attach to one creature.
      - text: "## Exact Paladin distinction For this spell only, **Banishment is planar/extradimensional displacement and is not teleportation**."
      - text: "- **No Escape** forbids voluntary teleportation, invisibility **or extradimensional travel**. It therefore blocks ACCEPT, whether the target elects failure or rolls and fails. It does not block a genuinely unwilling RESIST target that fails. - **Verdict Absolute** forbids voluntary teleportation or invisibility only. It does **not** block this non-teleport Banishment. - **Reign of Ashes** prevents benefit from Dash or teleportation. It does **not** block this non-teleport Banishment."
      - text: This classification is Banishment-only and does not redefine other planar travel.
      - text: "## The temporary cell On successful establishment, the same target identity and its legal noncreature worn/carried gear move to a generated extraplanar spell-cell. The cell is not identified with any named plane, afterlife, deity, Heartcell state or soul destination."
      - text: Cell-local x is east, y north, z up; floor is z=0. The primary starts centered at local (0,0).
      - text: Initial horizontal extent is the current footprint plus 5 feet clearance on every side, rounded upward to the next 5 feet. Height is current occupied body height plus 5 feet overhead, rounded upward to the next 5 feet. A Medium 5x5 body of occupied height 6 feet therefore starts in **x/y -7.5..+7.5, z 0..15**.
      - text: The cell floor is ephemeral support. It supplies ordinary light and a nonharmful sustaining medium, but no harvestable air/water/material, resistance, immunity or biology.
      - text: "### Geometry inside A voluntary size/form change must already fit the current cell before payment under its actual body/source rules. Banishment cannot expand first to legalize the cast."
      - text: A **mandatory** death, source expiry, summon ending, actual-0-HP ending, Polymorph reversion or other mandatory body event always occurs. Banishment never cancels or delays it. If the resulting body needs more room, the cell expands **after** that mandatory event.
      - text: "## Visitors and created creatures A different creature can enter only through an independently legal source that can truly identify/reach this cell and target a fitting footprint already inside the current opaque boundary."
      - text: An occupant may summon/create a creature only into a fitting unoccupied footprint it can currently perceive/target **before** casting. No pre-cast expansion supplies missing space or line of effect. After a body legally establishes, the cell may expand only for ordinary clearance.
      - text: Every visitor/summon keeps its own real Action/RP/frequency/concentration/command/deadline rules.
      - text: "## Time and agency Successful establishment records an absolute deadline **60 elapsed seconds later**. Existing HP, Wounds, conditions, death saves, concentration, summon timers and other real clocks continue normally. Banishment itself adds no Incapacitated, healing, rest or stasis."
      - text: The application ends early when its concentration ends, when a legal Xi interaction ends the actual target branch, when the primary legally escapes, or when this Banishment application is actually ended/suppressed under its current rule.
      - text: "## Legal escape An occupant may use an independently legal travel source, paying its real costs and meeting its own destination, perception, geometry and blocker rules."
      - text: If the **primary** legally escapes, its Banishment application ends immediately. The spell then settles every remaining secondary body/object and destroys the cell in that same resolution sequence. The escaped primary is never pulled back later.
      - text: "## Closure and physical placement When Banishment ends, every remaining real identity is mandatorily returned to the **same snapshotted origin plane**. The primary uses its fixed world origin anchor; secondary bodies use deterministic anchors based on their in-cell offsets. The cell then ends."
      - text: A current **Dimensional Anchor** or other true endpoint blocker is respected for ordinary establishment/escape. At terminal Banishment closure only, a NEW narrow exception ignores **only** such transport-prevention if necessary to complete the mandatory return. It does not ignore walls, occupied space, anatomy, death, missing bodies or item existence and grants no later bypass.
      - text: "After the plane return, physical fitting uses the existing body rule: 1. normal current-anatomy space at the same anchor; 2. a legal squeeze; 3. nearest legal adjacent placement in the same unobstructed region, using minimum displacement, greatest overlap, then fixed neutral board order."
      - text: It never searches farther afield, passes through a wall/closed barrier, crushes another body or invents damage.
      - text: "### Placement conflict If none of those physical placements fits, **Banishment still ends** and the cell is destroyed. The same identity is now on the origin plane with a canonical **placement conflict** at its fixed anchor. It has no legal occupied footprint until the obstruction is removed or an independently legal relocation effect resolves the conflict."
      - text: This is not continuing exile, deletion, overlap permission, stasis, Incapacitated or a new condition. Actual HP, conditions, death/body rules and clocks continue. Spatial actions still need their ordinary position/path/reach/perception/line-of-effect prerequisites; while no legal footprint exists, those prerequisites normally cannot be satisfied.
      - text: If a legal returned footprint is unsupported, ordinary Falling begins after actual placement. Banishment does not move a falling body to the ground.
      - text: Loose surviving items use the existing item-placement rule. If no legal item drop exists, the item remains real with placement pending.

    - generic: "['sources/DescentReviewChanges/ReviewBible/docs/mechanics/CONTROL_STATES_PLAYTEST.md', 'sources/DescentReviewChanges/ReviewBible/docs/mechanics/ENCHANTMENT_CONTROL_INTEGRATION_PLAYTEST.md'] sources/DescentReviewChanges/ReviewBible/docs/mechanics/CONTROL_STATES_PLAYTEST.md # Source-qualified control states **REVIEWED / reference-VALIDATED — supplied scope only.** This guide binds the supplied 219 source passages (179 initial candidates and 40 supplemental cards). It does not claim a complete semantic audit of every class, spell, creature or item, and does not close DD16 or DD18. Original source objects remain unchanged. NEW below marks an integration decision, not text recovered from a historical book. Use this with [Core conditions](CONDITIONS_PLAYTEST.md), [custom riders](CUSTOM_CONDITION_BINDINGS.md), [combat timing](COMBAT_ACTION_PLAYTEST.md), [Strain](STRAIN_PLAYTEST.md), [source bindings](CONTROL_SOURCE_BINDINGS.md), and the [coverage ledger](CONTROL_STATE_COVERAGE.md). Exact structured records are in `data/playtest/control_state_bindings.json`. The source procedure supplies its costs, application triggers, target eligibility, saves, frequency, concentration and endings; a component ID alone grants nothing. Alternative modes never apply together merely because both IDs appear in a binding. ## Records, clocks and precedence Each application records its identity, exact source, owner character, target, selected mode/components, application order, recorded source DC, deadline/marker, concentration or attachment relationship, remaining uses, and applicable immunity/suppression. Display names never create conditions or prerequisites. An owner's mark cannot satisfy another owner's class hook. A corpse marker, map point, archived spell, intrinsic Oracle Curse and hard-denial history are distinct state types. Explicit operative effect text controls over a less-specific summary duration field. Use preserved regular-turn markers for **next** turn and bare N-round durations as in the combat guide; an in-progress current turn is not the next turn. Unqualified next turn refers to the source; explicit its/target refers to the target. Bare hostile N rounds, including hostile marks, use N future target regular ends. Beneficial/self N rounds use source regular starts. Preserve virtual markers through initiative changes and absence. Out of initiative bare N rounds use application+6N seconds, retaining that deadline on entering initiative. Explicit source/concentration endings still apply. **NEW current-turn bridge:** “end of that turn” or the supplied bare “end turn” effects end at the end of the actual turn containing the application event, including another creature's or an extra turn. With no current turn, use application+6 seconds. An application during end-turn resolution expires at that same end after resolving, rather than acquiring a fabricated next turn. This applies to Resonant Seed, Winter's Embrace, Frost Totem and Glue. It does not override an explicit next-turn endpoint. The reference represents an expiry as an ordered `(seconds, phase)` marker, preserving exact rational seconds from the combat clock. Every turn-end deadline uses expiry phase 20: current actual turn, source/target next regular end, Nth future target regular end, and Balanced Elements' following target end. End-trigger application/resolution occurs at phase 10 and the next event slot at phase 30. The effect therefore remains during final end events and expires afterward at that same second. Turn-start and absolute elapsed deadlines use phase 0. Callers must choose an explicit typed deadline; bare numeric expiries are rejected. No fractional-second workaround or duplicate turn is created. Repeat saves, ordinary duration expiry and previously reviewed periodic/recharge clocks use regular turns unless an explicit exception applies. This is **not** a global rule changing every “each turn” damage trigger. Zone entry/start application checks, repeat ending saves and once-per-numbered-round caps are distinct events. Follow each source's actual trigger and cap; concentration alone grants no recurring application save. ## Speed arbitration **NEW:** Keep soft flat reductions, soft fractional reductions, explicit Speed0 controls, and Core Grappled/Restrained separate. Terrain changes movement cost; it is not a Speed penalty. “Hostile soft control” means an adverse control record regardless of whether an enemy, ally or the victim applied it. 1. Determine each possessed mode's Speed after compatible ordinary bonuses and systemic flat penalties (including serum/expedition Strain, serum side effects, cargo and stances). Their existing stacking rules still apply. 2. Evaluate every unsuppressed adverse soft-control record against that **same** pre-control Speed. Flat -10 gives `max(0, Speed-10)`; half gives `floor(Speed/2)`; use the exact registered formula for other records. 3. Use the lowest result. Different slows do not add unless an expressly reviewed source grants an exception. Keep all losing records and their independent deadlines. 4. Apply separately established global multipliers such as Exhaustion's half-Speed. Apply an active Hypermutation floor as specified below. 5. An explicit Speed0 state, Core Grappled/Restrained, Exhaustion5 or another actual immobilization overrides the numeric result. An unavailable mode remains unavailable. A separate prohibition on voluntary movement is not removed by a positive Speed. Thus -10 and half on Speed30 yield15; Exhaustion then yields7. Two -10 records yield20, not10. Serum/expedition penalties remain independent system penalties, not competing hostile slow records. A minimum attached to a penalty cannot raise existing Speed: Iron Leash Aura uses `min(preSpeed, max(5, preSpeed-10))`. Active adverse soft controls and explicit source Speed0 controls carry `eligibility.slowed-foe`; Core Grappled/Restrained alone, Prone, cargo, terrain and Exhaustion do not. A numerically overshadowed application still qualifies. Suppressed, expired or prevented applications do not. Same owner/target/group/exact profile refreshes without adding magnitude or shortening its remaining deadline. Different magnitude/formula profiles retain separate deadlines within a group; a weaker later application cannot erase an unexpired stronger one. Removing/suppressing one record exposes any other valid record. Paid reapplication and source caps remain required. No arithmetic Speed result grants movement: the combat guide's common spent-movement ledger still applies. ## Mage Cold and elemental extension One owner group `mage.cold-focus:<owner>` contains passive/Bolt/Surge flat profiles and Glacier profiles. A single hit invoking both the passive and Bolt/Surge does not create duplicate penalties. The current Cold Focus passive requires positive actual cold damage from a qualifying Cold Mage spell. Bolt/Surge explicitly apply their Cold rider on a legal hit even if damage is reduced to0. Glacier's failed-save profile does not require positive damage. A successful Glacier save can still trigger the passive if it deals positive cold damage and Cold is the current Focus. | Candidate | Ordinary result | With **Arch** Eleme sources/DescentReviewChanges/ReviewBible/docs/mechanics/ENCHANTMENT_CONTROL_INTEGRATION_PLAYTEST.md # Enchantment control integration — provisional playtest REVIEWED PROVISIONAL MANUSCRIPT. These NEW integration rules accompany seven approved rank-two Enchantment cards. The [exact review](../../../../docs/audit/2026-10-08/full-game/GPT_ENCHANTMENT_RANK2_EXACT_REVIEW.md) approves the submitted body and records the binding interpretations below. Source originals remain preserved. This is not runtime certification, human balance approval, whole-game closure or GitHub delivery approval. ## Ordinary compelled Attack Actions When an actual compulsion orders an ordinary Attack Action, use the target's native ordinary attack sequence, including inherent Extra Attack and an NPC's native Multiattack if that is actually its ordinary Action. No additional Action/attack is granted. A source expressly limited to one attack retains that limit: Confusing Flash is such an exception. An ordinary attack instruction is not permission to invent an Attack Action or convert another source's single attack into Multiattack. Spend required ordinary ammunition normally. The command cannot activate consumable magic, special ammunition with a distinct expendable feature, RP/spellcasting, charges, recharge/per-rest abilities or optional finite-resource riders. Actual optional expenditures remain optional and cannot be compelled indirectly by choosing a method which requires them. Where instruction wording leaves the choice open, the target/controller may use a different legal ordinary method. If none satisfies the instruction, that instruction fails without spending a prohibited resource or manufacturing one. Existing source-specific limits and actual absolute prohibitions remain. ## Application IDs and independently scoped components Each cast has a distinct application ID. Each component retains its own exact source/caster/target, application identity, deadline and actual defense state. Replacing one component never silently replaces another. For Dire Charm, successfully established Charmed replaces only that caster's previous Dire Charm Charmed relation. Successfully established servant control replaces only the existing Dire Charm servant component across casters. Charmed-only and servant-only casts can therefore coexist. A successful repeat save, expiry or removal clears only still-active components originating from that application; an old application's save cannot remove a replacement component from another cast. Empty applications have no remaining save schedule. Core Charmed from other sources is unaffected. Shared hard-control history never refunds, even if a component is suppressed or replaced before acting. ## Compelled budget arbitration This NEW rule resolves collisions among actual active mandatory instructions, including Command, Confusing Flash, Illusive Commands, Dire Charm and Crown of Madness. It does not create commands, extend duration, add repeated control to a one-turn source, grant new target permissions or override an explicit source-specific exception. Record a priority timestamp for each specific instruction: - Command, Confusing Flash and Illusive Commands: successful establishment of that failed-save instruction. - Dire Charm and Crown of Madness: issue of that turn's particular valid order, not the age of the underlying spell alone. - Simultaneous instructions: underlying effect application order, then a stable source/mechanical ID as the neutral tie-break. Record it before resolving choices; do not decide a favorable order after outcomes. At execution, gather still-active mandatory instructions and consider earlier priority first. A currently impossible/ineligible instruction claims no budget; proceed to the next. Compatible requirements can share one genuine legal execution. A single Action satisfies two instructions only if that same Action actually fulfills both. Once an earlier valid instruction claims an indivisible ordinary Action/interaction, an incompatible later instruction receives no second copy. Movement uses only the actual remaining movement allowance; sequencing instructions cannot reset spent movement or violate an earlier still-maintained posture/restriction. A losing ongoing instruction is ineffective only for its contested budget/opportunity, not automatically dispelled. Its compatible independent portions may still operate within existing permissions. Its own endpoint, replacement and saves remain. Priority loss never refunds hard-control history. An invalid later order cannot erase an earlier mandate merely by being issued. Example: Grovel and an Attack Action may both be satisfied if Prone uses only legal movement/posture and the attack remains legal. Two incompatible ordinary Actions cannot both be demanded from one Action budget. This arbitrates actual compelled behavior; it does not make compulsion optional or restore unavailable actions. ## Six-second control cadence outside initiative This NEW cadence binds Dire Charm and Crown of Madness's ongoing order/end-save procedures. Other sources retain their actual timing unless expressly bound by a further review. For a target with no existing initiative/control cycle, successful outside-initiative application begins a six-second virtual target cycle: first START immediately after resolution, first END six elapsed seconds later, then repeated six-second cycles. END resolves before the next cycle's START at the same boundary. Maximum duration remains the original absolute60-second deadline; expiry can end the spell before a later opportunity. Reviewed procedure: a target shares one active virtual control cycle and one ordinary behavior budget across concurrent control applications. A new application during an already-open virtual cycle does not replay START or replenish budgets; its specifically allowed current-cycle END save still occurs. Each spell retains its own maximum deadline, components and saves. This treats application during an outside-initiative cycle like application during an existing regular turn, preventing repeated casts from creating extra immediate Actions. The cycle supplies the bound spells' order opportunities, one ordinary six-second behavior budget where needed, and their repeat saves. It does not refresh encounter Reactions, recharge, resources, limited uses or manufacture extra combat turns. If actual combat order becomes necessary, establish ordinary initiative before any not-yet-committed hostile behavior; a virtual cycle is no permission for an extra pre-initiative attack. Reviewed procedure for entering initiative: map the currently open virtual cycle to the target's first actual regular turn. Carry already received order opportunities and spent ordinary Action/movement/interaction budgets into that turn; do not replay or refill them at its START. The next still-unresolved END save occurs at that actual turn's END. Thereafter use actual regular starts/ends for future triggers. Keep the original absolute du"

Finite/reactive prevention such as Bloom and Ancestral Guardian follows those defenses before post-defense transfer and temporary HP. Within the same stage, select eligible positive mandatory/automatic protection before optional offers; competing mandatory claims are chosen by the original damage target. A legally chosen Bloodbond/Veinlock operation can therefore preempt later Aegis/Final Stand because that root is already claimed. Do not preview or consume defenses/Bloom to decide a later-stage preference. The helper accepts an already selected legal operation; it does not select Reactions or schedule automatic offers. Optional transfer requires a positive chosen amount and available damage. Zero transfer cannot spend an optional Reaction/resource/use. Original actions, range, willingness, costs, active-state requirements, cadence and source-specific use limits still apply. ## Hit identity and final damage accounting The original damage target retains its original hit, failed-save, critical and on-hit-rider identity even if all damage is transferred. A rider that requires positive damage must recheck the actual remaining packet. A transferred recipient was not hit by that attack and did not fail its save; it receives no attached poison/control/maiming/knockout rider merely because damage moved. Accordingly, transferred damage from a critical attack gives an already0HP recipient the ordinary one damage failure, not two critical-hit failures. Concentration still sees the transferred recipient's resolved packet even if temporary HP absorbs all of it. Use the [reviewed life-state interface](HEALING_DEATH_PLAYTEST.md) after resolving each final recipient. Numeric actual damage is temporary HP absorbed plus actual normal HP lost, excluding overkill and damage prevented by an HP floor. Sum each terminal recipient once. Do not also count the unsplit parent or unrelated counterdamage. Physical root totals include every recipient. A healing/resource/reflection consumer sums only branches permitted for that consumer and qualifying for its own source's target predicate. For example, a meaningful-hostile-only drain does not automatically count damage transferred onto a friendly protector. Verify the available Action/Bonus Action/Reaction, its exact trigger, components, targets, range and line of effect. No legal target means no activation or payment. A target in an area still needs the card's eligibility. Total cover blocks direct targeting unless expressly overridden. 2. Choose a spell from your actual repertoire. Determine its class-specific rank and RP cost: rank 0–9 costs **0/2/3/5/7/10/13/17/21/26**. Add explicitly printed extra costs; Resonance Shield adds 1 RP even when the caster receives it as a cantrip. Generic Overcast is excluded. 3. Read Dissonance **before** casting. Limit = max(4, 2 + PB + max(0, casting modifier)). At limit/limit+3/limit+6, ranked spells cost +1/+2/+3 RP. At limit+6 ordinary rank-1+ casting is blocked. An ordinary missed, saved-against or countered legal cast refunds nothing and never restores displaced concentration. 5. First cast or a different name adds +0. Apply the class's exact protected reduction, routing or vent and any hook whose printed trigger is beginning/casting. Weapon or idle turns do not reset the previous name/Refrain unless the class express PAT Dissonance 809 # Spell integration rules — provisional v0.1 Use this document with the [shared class rules](CLASS_PLAYTEST_RULES.md), the class's own engine and the [spell packet](SPELL_PLAYTEST_STARTER.md). This is a staged development layer, not an approved-book replacement or a live companion update. ## What owns each decision | Decision | Source used for this packet | |---|---| | Spell identity, every school placement and per-class rank/access | Preserved `src/data/spells.js`; do not collapse different class ranks into one global rank. | | Common RP prices and additional spell evidence | Core Systems Known Descent V2. Its spell blocks are retained with the catalog summaries. | | Class identity, engine, spellbook/preparation and cadence exceptions | The current class playtest packet. | | Generic Dissonance/Refrain, action limits and core conditions | Shared class playtest rules; source Core conditions remain unchanged. | | Missing targeting, exact endpoints, concentration ambiguities and exploit limits | Explicitly provisional decisions on each spell card. | The approved Core V2 source has 936 spell blocks. The companion catalog has 848 unique IDs with 936 school placements, but leaves four metadata fields unspecified. These are different views of the source, not evidence that all casting details were absent. Core's generic “Automatic or opposed as described” does not supply a concrete save or targeting rule. Eleven possible renamed entries remain comparative evidence in the register. Harmonic Bulwark's catalog summary says 5 temporary HP per RP while the possible historical Mana Shield entry says 2; its [individual transformation and buffer review](TRANSFORMATION_SPELL_PLAYTEST.md) now supplies the operational decision. Both source variants remain preserved. Other unresolved aliases stay pending their own review; matching names never silently merge effects. Resonance Kindle now has [exact manuscript approval](../../../../docs/audit/2026-10-08/full-game/GPT_CONJURATION_CANTRIP_EXACT_REVIEW.md): Action/Self/V/S, recover 1 RP only when this spell reaches resolution. Its one character-wide use is spent at legal cast start and refreshes only on a beneficial completed long rest. The comparative Mana Kindle block remains separately preserved; its broader wording does not trigger recovery from other cantrips and its historical range grants no remote recipient. The [Conjuration cantrip status](CONJURATION_CANTRIP_REVIEW_STATUS.json) records this bounded decision and the other eleven approved cards. ## Select a spell 1. Choose the class through which you are learning/casting it. Check that class's school access and minimum **class** level on the card. 2. Use that class's spell rank and Core V2 cost. A different eligible class may receive the same spell at a different rank and level. 3. Record it in your allowed known, prepared or spellbook choices. An eligible catalog entry is not an automatic grant. Wizard and Druid keep their explicit preparation systems; fallback counts apply only where the class has no more specific rule. 4. Select only a spell marked `operational-playtest-draft` in this packet, or an operational spell expressly supplied by a class card. Other records stay visible in the unfinished queue. 5. A class card and an ordinary spell with the same name remain separate records with separate acquisition and costs. Knowing one does not grant the other. Oath spells, Matrix techniques and similar class spells use their own card. Oracle uses RC and its Mystery/Curse system. Soldier and Warrior have no general Weave access. Dragoon first gains ordinary magic at class 3; Paladin at class 2. Sufficient RP, total character level, a class feature with the same name, or a borrowed spellbook cannot bypass a gate. ## Resolve a cast at the table 1. Verify the available Action/Bonus Action/Reaction, its exact trigger, components, targets, range and line of effect. No legal target means no activation or payment. A target in an area still needs the card's eligibility. Total cover blocks direct targeting unless expressly overridden. 2. Choose a spell from your actual repertoire. Determine its class-specific rank and RP cost: rank 0–9 costs **0/2/3/5/7/10/13/17/21/26**. Add explicitly printed extra costs; Resonance Shield adds 1 RP even when the caster receives it as a cantrip. Generic Overcast is excluded. 3. Read Dissonance **before** casting. Limit = max(4, 2 + PB + max(0, casting modifier)). At limit/limit+3/limit+6, ranked spells cost +1/+2/+3 RP. At limit+6 ordinary rank-1+ casting is blocked. Cantrips have no strain surcharge but still build Refrain. An expressly granted traumatic-casting exception pays the cost and its printed trauma; it grants neither rank nor repertoire access. 4. Commit the legal cast's RP, materials and other printed costs before any attack/save or interruption. Perform its components and establish concentration if required, ending incompatible prior concentration. An ordinary missed, saved-against or countered legal cast refunds nothing and never restores displaced concentration. 5. At this cast's beginning, apply ordinary Dissonance and Refrain once: 0 base for rank 0, 1 for ranks 1–5, 2 for ranks 6–9; consecutive same-named casts add Refrain +1/+2/+3, capped at +3. First cast or a different name adds +0. Apply the class's exact protected reduction, routing or vent and any hook whose printed trigger is beginning/casting. Weapon or idle turns do not reset the previous name/Refrain unless the class expressly says so. This chronological commitment occurs before the Counterspell window; a later Reaction cast updates its own history, and resuming the earlier cast does not update that history again. 6. Open legal cast-interruption Reactions, resolving nest PAT cost 2753 ision. Both source variants remain preserved. Other unresolved aliases stay pending their own review; matching names never silently merge effects. Sufficient RP, total character level, a class feature with the same name, or a borrowed spellbook cannot bypass a gate. ## Resolve a cast at the table 1. Verify the available Action/Bonus Action/Reaction, its exact trigger, components, targets, range and line of effect. No legal target means no activation or payment. A target in an area still needs the card's eligibility. Total cover blocks direct targeting unless expressly overridden. 2. Choose a spell from your actual repertoire. Determine its class-specific rank and RP cost: rank 0–9 costs **0/2/3/5/7/10/13/17/21/26**. Add explicitly printed extra costs; Resonance Shield adds 1 RP even when the caster receives it as a cantrip. Generic Overcast is excluded. 3. Read Dissonance **before** casting. Limit = max(4, 2 + PB + max(0, casting modifier)). At limit/limit+3/limit+6, ranked spells cost +1/+2/+3 RP. At limit+6 ordinary rank-1+ casting is blocked. Cantrips have no strain surcharge but still build Refrain. An expressly granted traumatic-casting exception pays the cost and its printed trauma; it grants neither rank nor repertoire access. 4. Commit the legal cast's RP, materials and other printed costs before any attack/save or interruption. Perform its components and establish concentration if required, ending incompatible prior concentration. An ordinary missed, saved-against or countered legal cast refunds nothing and never restores displaced concentration. 5. At this cast's beginning, apply ordinary Dissonance and Refrain once: 0 base for rank 0, 1 for ranks 1–5, 2 for ranks 6–9; consecutive same-named casts add Refrain +1/+2/+3, capped at +3. First cast or a different name adds +0. Apply the class's exact protected reduction, routing or vent and any hook whose printed trigger is beginning/casting. Weapon or idle turns do not reset the previous name/Refrain unless the class expressly says so. This chronological commitment occurs before the Counterspell window; a later Reaction cast updates its own history, and resuming the earlier cast does not update that history again. 6. Open legal cast-interruption Reactions, resolving nested counters last-in, first-out. A surviving cast then resolves its effect: spell attacks are d20 + PB + the actual casting ability modifier; save DC is 8 + PB + that modifier. Use the exact card's hit/failure/success results and ordinary damage/conditions. Outcome-dependent class hooks occur only at their printed timing and with the required actual result. No automatic cantrip scaling applies. A negated spell grants no success/hit/damage effect merely because its casting cost was paid. 7. Record concentration, exact expiration, per-rest use, summon and companion command obligations. A second spell cannot supply an ungranted Action, duplicate concentration or resource reset. A readied spell commits this casting sequence at preparation; release has no second cast, cost, cadence update or Counterspell window. Countering requires an actual operational spell or class card, its repertoire/access and a legal remaining Reaction. The ordinary Counterspell is now reviewed; it is separate from the Mage and Wizard class cards. Dispel Minor, Dispel Resonance, Dispel Magic (Lesser) and Dispel Magic use their exact individual scopes. Rank-limited dispels inspect the original generating cast's spell rank, not RP cost or the dispeller's rank. Higher and unassigned source variants retain their own unfinished status. ## Counterspell checks and interruption The [exact notebook review](../../../../docs/audit/2026-10-08/full-game/GPT_ABJURATION_RANK2_3_EXACT_REVIEW.md) confirms this authored timing interface and corrects the earlier binary-check proposal using the existing [Core roll rules](CORE_RESOLUTION_PLAYTEST.md). This is a tabletop procedure; the pricing helper does not implement nested reactions. Ordinary Counterspell uses its casting class's actual spellcasting ability + PB against **DC 10 + twice the triggering spell's actual rank**. The class Wizard/Mage cards retain their printed Intelligence + PB check and PAT Resonance Influence 42670 ion caster still affects that caster's own effective orientation normally. If Lock/Anchor is applied while Purge or Suggestion is already effective, it snapshots the currently effective defined value; later ending/suppression of the lower overlay does not alter that snapshot. When Lock/Anchor ends or is inactive because a zone was exited/suppressed, the then-live lower lawful result becomes effective immediately with its original deadline/history; no old snapshot is restored and no missed application replays. Undefined axes are never fabricated. The [complete fifteen-spell AC4 chapter](../spells/candidates/ABJURATION_CONJURATION_RANK4_REVIEWED_03.md) and [review receipt](../../../../docs/audit/2026-10-08/full-game/AC4_EXACT_ACCEPTANCE_03_RECEIPT.json) govern the exact accepted authored procedures, including separate Alignment Anchor placements, finite summon profiles and all scoped interfaces. These manuscript acceptances do not enable runtime selection or certify human balance. **Resonance Influence:** compute ordinary legal spell price first, including mandatory extras, Dissonance, eligible source reductions/waivers and each feature's own minimum. Then apply final plus/minus1, minimum0. A feature's minimum1 reduction step is not a universal final-price floor. Ease can offset one RP of the resulting total, including a mandatory Resonance Shield extra. A genuine RP-priced spell reduced/waived to0 still qualifies; Burden makes it1. In Mage Spell Sharing, the Mage's earlier sharing payment is unchanged, while the ally's later actual shared spell is independently priced: Ease0/Burden1. An ally unable to pay cannot commit, leaves Influence pending, and still uses the original limited sharing window. Nonspell techniques and charge-only spells without an RP-price interface do not qualify merely because they have costs. Qualifying class discounts require their actual class/spell eligibility; examples do not reclassify nonspell techniques. No runtime implementation or numerical balance is certified by this manuscript ruling. ## Rank-two Enchantment and shared control integration Seven rank-two Enchantment identities have [exact manuscript approval](../../../../docs/audit/2026-10-08/full-game/GPT_ENCHANTMENT_RANK2_EXACT_REVIEW.md) with no functional card repairs. Their [shared control integration](ENCHANTMENT_CONTROL_INTEGRATION_PLAYTEST.md) is part of this provisional rules overlay and applies its specific arbitration interface to existing Command and Confusing Flash as well as Illusive Commands, Dire Charm and Crown of Madness. Ordinary real-turn start processing still occurs. One-shot commands keep their own timing while sharing actual remaining budgets. Calm Emotions and Zone of Truth keep independent first-exposure save histories in fixed zones, with total-cover/outside suppression and return without rerolling. Calm Emotions suppresses actual Frightened effects while underlying clocks and saves continue. The five compared emotion-themed class cards acquire no new maintenance dependency. Zone of Truth preserves both actual class/rank/price modes without granting additional access or a truth-detection oracle.     - generic: "# Create and advance a character **REVIEWED CHARACTER FOUNDATION — authored operational PLAYTEST candidate.** The [source conference](../../../../docs/audit/2026-10-08/full-game/GPT_CHARACTER_FOUNDATION_CONFERENCE.md) and [exact review](../../../../docs/audit/2026-10-08/full-game/GPT_CHARACTER_FOUNDATION_EXACT_REVIEW.md), with both requested wording repairs applied, settle the class foundations and advancement rules below. Use the [17-class tables](CHARACTER_FOUNDATION_TABLES.md) beside this guide. Use the later reviewed ancestry/background and equipment guides for their stated scopes, and the reviewed language chapter for language grants and records. Remaining option dependencies and live companion parity are still separate work. ## Begin a character sheet 1. **Record the concept and first Base class.** Choose one of the 17 classes. Start at character level 1 and that class’s level 1. Keep a chronological level record and the order in which you enter classes. Arch and Hero are later stages within a class, not additional Base classes. The former Fighter/Swashbuckler entries and separate historical prestige records supply no extra starting class or automatic grants. 2. **Generate six abilities.** Choose the method before generating scores: roll four d6, discard the lowest and total the other three, six times; or assign the standard array 15, 14, 13, 12, 10, 8. Assign those six results once to STR, DEX, CON, INT, WIS and CHA. Keep the original rolls. Do not reroll, mix methods or select a preferred set without a separate table rule. The optional provisional point-buy method below is also available. 3. **Record ancestry and any required variant.** Apply its actual ability increases once after assigning scores, resolving an OR choice once. Record size, movement, senses, languages and each complete trait procedure from its source. Trait names alone are insufficient. Do not stack alternative builder/racial-variant entries onto the same book trait. Use the reviewed ancestry/background integration and complete source bindings for all14 base ancestries and45 variants; apply the reviewed language chapter to their actual language grants. This step grants no authority to revise their cosmology or origin lore. 4. **Choose one background.** Record its actual skills, tools, languages, feature and starting package, including required choices. Use the reviewed ancestry/background guide and complete source bindings for the20 background packages; its named outstanding interfaces remain outstanding. The source permits a custom background with two skills, two tools/languages, equipment and a Reputation-related feature developed with the Director; that permission does not supply an invented unlimited feature or a second background. 5. **Apply starting training.** Use this first class’s saves, armor, weapons, tools and number of skill choices from the foundation tables. Use the class list for each skill choice. Add ancestry/background training with the duplicate procedure below. Proficiency grants training, not equipment ownership, spells or a second PB on one roll. 6. **Set HP, Hit Dice and proficiency.** First-character-level base HP is the maximum face of this class’s HD; add current CON modifier, minimum 1. Record one unspent Hit Die of that size. At level 1 PB is +2. Keep base maximum HP separate from later effects, reductions and systemic multipliers. 7. **Acquire the actual class kit.** Follow the class’s own level-1 engine, automatic grants and scheduled choices. Ordinary classes start with four Base choices, at least two active, where their schedule says so. Druid, Oracle and Paladin have explicit schedules; follow those instead. Make required engine/path choices, such as Oracle Mystery and Curse, exactly when their cards require them. Use the [class library](CLASS_PLAYTEST_INDEX.md), [legal acquisition examples](CLASS_PLAYTEST_BUILD_EXAMPLES.md), and [Paladin oath pairs](PALADIN_OATHS_AND_OATHBREAKERS.md). No legacy Skill Points, companion feature strings or historical prestige rows add grants to this schedule. 8. **Record magic and class resources.** Use the class’s current Core V2 RP table and rank gates, plus its actual known/prepared/spellbook procedure. Record each selected operational spell’s identity and casting class. Enough RP never unlocks a rank or school. Oracle uses Revelation Charges; Soldier and Warrior have no general RP casting. Initial class resources and empty stores follow their actual engine setup; preparation or copying is not waived. Read [shared class rules](CLASS_PLAYTEST_RULES.md) and [spell integration](SPELL_INTEGRATION_RULES.md). 9. **Choose starting equipment or money.** Take the class and background packages, **or** roll the first class’s starting wealth and buy equipment with it. Wealth replaces all starting package equipment, including package money; background training and features remain. Record purchases, remaining coins, ammunition, components, containers, quantities and actual weight/Load. An item must exist with usable statistics; a proficiency or feature label cannot create it. Use the reviewed equipment/acquisition guide, complete item procedures and starting-package records; keep any actually unresolved entry visible. 10. **Finish the derived sheet.** Calculate ability modifiers, trained saves and skills, legal equipped AC, movement, weapon attacks, class attacks/DCs and actual resource caps. Choose one applicable AC formula rather than adding formulas. A normal proficient attack is d20 + PB + its actual attack modifier; class DC is 8 + PB + its actual class modifier. Use actual negative modifiers; the class card’s minimum-1 effect modifier is not a minimum attack/DC modifier. Note conditions, Wounds, supplies and other scene state separately. Record the source/card for every nonordinary number. An ancestry/background feature, spell or item that remains mechanically unresolved is a visible unfinished dependency. A checked box or a saved companion character does not resolve that dependency. ## Abilities and training Ability modifier = floor((score − 10) / 2), including negative results. For example, 8 gives −1, 13 gives +1 and 16 gives +3. **Optional new candidate method:** 27-point buy, scores 8–15 before ancestry. Costs are 8:0, 9:1, 10:2, 11:3, 12:4, 13:5, 14:7, 15:9. Spend no more than 27 points across six scores; no score outside that range. This adopts the existing companion method as a provisional option, not a claim that the book already specified it. Resolve duplicate training as follows: - For a selectable grant, choose another eligible option from that grant’s own list. - For a fixed ancestry/background skill grant with no alternative list, choose another of the 20 basic skills. - For a duplicated tool, choose another eligible tool from that grant’s list; if it has no list, use another actually supported tool in the same broad category. - Duplicate armor, weapon, save or multiclass chassis training gives no skill or feat compensation. A new multiclass skill selection must still use its own permitted list. - Duplicate proficiency never creates Expertise unless an exact feature says so. If a proficiency grant requires a choice and every legal option for that grant is already possessed, that grant provides no additional proficiency. Character creation, multiclass entry or advancement remains legal. Do not substitute a feat, Expertise, another category of proficiency, an unrelated skill/tool, or a banked future selection. This applies to skill and tool lists, including a legal replacement list or category that is itself exhausted. PB is applied once to a check even if a skill and tool both qualify. An exact Expertise multiplier modifies that one proficiency contribution; it does not add another independent PB. ## Gain a level Opening campaigns officially use milestones. The Director’s announced milestones grant advancement; a CR-to-XP UI helper is not the official Opening reward law. The book’s cumulative XP thresholds remain available for tables using optional XP. See [XP provenance](XP_ADVANCEMENT_CANON.md). Each earned advancement adds one total character level, to a combined maximum of 20. Choose one existing Base class to advance, or enter one new Base class if eligible. Process multiple earned levels one at a time; do not skip intervening choices. 1. Record the gained class and new class level. Recalculate total level and PB: +2 at total 1–4, +3 at 5–8, +4 at 9–12, +5 at 13–16, +6 at 17–20. 2. Add one new unspent Hit Die of the gained class’s size. Previously spent Hit Dice remain spent; gaining this die does not refresh any earlier die. For HP, roll that die or use its fixed rounded-up average: d6→4, d8→5, d10→6, d12→7. Record the base result before CON. This includes taking a new class’s first level after character level 1; maximum first-level HP is never granted again. 3. Apply exactly the gained class’s acquisition row, automatic improvements and class-level prerequisites. Complete its choices and any newly reached Arch/Hero gate. Automatic grants, scheduled selections and ASIs are distinct; do not pay a selection twice or grant an extra selection because the same feature appears in a legacy table. 4. At an ASI milestone, increase one score by 2 or two scores by 1, normally no higher than 20, or select one eligible operational feat. Ordinary milestones are class levels 4/8/12/16/19; Oracle uses 4/8/14/19. Follow any already-reviewed specific exception. No starting feat or repeatable feat is implied. [Eight complete feat options](FEAT_PLAYTEST_INDEX.md) do not make the other 327 complete. 5. Update legal repertoire, prepared capacity, class scaling and resource maxima at the gained **class** level. Use class-specific Druid preparation, Wizard spellbook/matrices and other explicit rules before generic acquisition defaults. New capacity does not provide an unnamed spell, an unearned choice, a filled Echo record or an extra prepared spell without the required preparation procedure. 6. Recompute base maximum HP from all attained-level records using current CON: sum(max(1, each stored base value + current CON modifier)). Then apply the separate [maximum-HP ledger](MAXIMUM_HP_PLAYTEST.md), including compatible bonuses, reductions and systemic factors. Do not feed an already modified maximum back in as the base. 7. Keep current HP and spent resources unchanged except for a downward clamp when a maximum falls. An ordinary level-up is not healing or a rest: it cannot revive a downed/dead character, erase Wounds or refill already spent Hit Dice, RP, RC, class pools, item charges or uses. Newly granted features still follow an explicit acquisition/initialization clause if they have one; “gain a maximum” alone does not mean “restore current.” **New HP completion:** each level’s HP contribution has a minimum of 1. Recompute those individual floors after permanent CON changes. This preserves retroactive CON adjustment without a naive level×modifier-change formula that can bypass a floor. Capacity increases grant no current HP; decreases can clamp current HP without damage/healing triggers, consistent with the reviewed maximum-HP interface. Temporary ability effects follow their own actual source rules rather than being silently treated as permanent advancement. No banked card selections, free retraining or free respec is introduced. Use an existing explicit replacement/retraining procedure where one exists. ## Multiclass entry and class features When entering a new Base class, meet the multiclass score prerequisites for **every held class and the new class**. The foundation tables give the exact source gates; a class’s descriptive primary ability is not a substitute. These requirements are checked on new-class entry. Losing a qualifying score later does not erase earned classes/features, though it can prevent entering another new class. Advancing a class already held is not a new-class entry test. Only the first character"

This covers mundane armor, weapons, tools, focuses, common gear, creation packages, object damage, repair and ordinary acquisition. Cosmology is deferred until after a successful authorized GitHub push and verification of its remote commit. ## Record possessions and permissions Record each physical item's identity, quantity, actual owner/custodian, location, size, equipment grade, condition, remaining contents/charges and any explicit source exception. Ownership grants no proficiency, spell access, recipe, attunement or class feature. Training grants no free item. A price is a baseline quote, not automatic shop stock or a guaranteed buyer. The source catalog retains unknown fields as null. Its printed price or weight can apply to a bundle, such as twenty arrows, rather than one unit. The operational projection records bundle size and unit values separately. Unarmed strike is an attack option, not an inventory object. Services and living mounts are not generic mundane objects with a weapon's durability. Use the actual equipped item and current legal class/ancestry/card permissions. A historical feature with the same name supplies no additional grant. Spells and items require their own real targets and actions. A flask cannot be thrown from inside inaccessible melded equipment. ## Armor and shields Choose one applicable AC formula. Light armor adds the full DEX modifier to its printed base. Medium adds `min(DEX modifier, 2)`, including a negative modifier. Heavy uses its fixed AC without positive or negative DEX. Explicit separate AC bonuses apply only when their own requirements are met. One usable equipped shield contributes its printed bonus: buckler +1, shield +2, tower shield +3. No second shield bonus. Scale mail's description adds no slashing resistance. Small/Medium and Tiny fitted ordinary items retain the listed price, mass and profile in this candidate; fit creates no unprinted benefit. **New Large sizing:** ordinary armor, shields and weapons use twice the baseline price, twice the weight and twice generic structural HP, with the same AC, damage, reach and properties unless an exact source says otherwise. A fixed creation package includes the necessary fitting without an added creation charge. Its sized baseline matters for replacement, resale and repair. On the wealth route, buy the fitted item at its actual sized price. Powerful Build changes only its expressly permitted carrying calculations, not item size or weapon damage. It does not resize equipment on transformation; the reviewed body/equipment rules govern that transition. ## Object damage and Sunder These are **new mundane object procedures** completing the Core Sunder interface. A target must be one actual separately tracked object or explicitly modeled separable component. Naming an armor strap, pin, lace, hinge or seam does not create a cheaper independent target. Construct joints remain subject to the creature's actual stat block. Sunder uses an actual legal weapon attack against one perceived reachable object/component, consuming one attack when taking the Attack Action. It grants no extra attack or free damage to a wearer. The attacker cannot target a hidden component through a creature or solid obstruction. Against worn/held gear, target AC is the higher of the material AC and wearer's current AC. Unattended gear uses material AC. Ordinary attack eligibility and printed object-target exclusions still apply. Worn/carried inventory is never damaged merely because its creature is hit. An attack, spell or hazard damages equipment only if its actual rule expressly targets/includes objects, structures, carried gear or that item.
